# Target Architecture — Completion Checklist

**Definition of done:** when every box below is checked, the target architecture in [PROPOSAL.md](PROPOSAL.md) is 100% reached — code layout, dependency graph, enforcement, and agent-facing guidance all match the specification, with zero standing `LAYER_MATRIX_EXCEPTIONS` and the §2.6 dead surface gone.

Conventions: every code item lands with its tests and its guidance updates in the same PR (house rule). Items tagged **[decision]** need an explicit Ben/Illia call first (listed in PROPOSAL §12.10). A checked box means the item landed on `main`; the PR that landed it is named inline. Verification commands are given where they are crisp; `cargo test -p ironclaw_architecture_tests` (→ `ironclaw_architecture_tests` after its rename) is implied after every structural PR.

**Refreshed 2026-07-30 against `origin/main` @ `457088c8f`.** There is no longer a gated workstream: the `[#6696]` tag is retired, WS9 is ungated with three of its five items already ticked, and several WS4/WS6 items landed with #6691. What the merges did *not* do is called out where it matters — the runner await-edge shed is still open and is now a design question, not a wait.

---

## WS1 — Contracts extraction (kills the 7 W4.3 exceptions + `auth→turns`)

- [x] Complete the turn vocabulary in `host_api::turn`: move `TurnStatus` and the remaining turn DTOs that vocabulary-only consumers need; delete `turns/src/ids.rs` + `scope.rs` + `turns::product_adapter` re-export shims; repoint the six vocabulary-only consumers (`auth`, `event_streams`, `outbound`, `telegram_extension`, `triggers`, `event_projections`) to `host_api`. **Landed with the WS1.1 PR (#6967).** `host_api::turn` absorbed `TurnStatus` (with its inseparable `GateKind`/`BlockedReason` gate correspondence — splitting them would have duplicated the single `GateKind -> TurnStatus` match table), plus `EventCursor` and `RunOriginAdapter`, which the six actually needed and which the lead sheet did not name. All three shims are gone: `ids.rs`, `scope.rs`, and the whole `turns/src/product_adapter/` module (its wildcard re-export died; its `fakes.rs` moved to `host_api::product_adapter::test_support::fakes`, beside the traits it implements, and `ironclaw_assistant` — the only consumer — re-points there). Deleting `ids.rs` forced the one real rename in the change: its `pub type GateRef = TurnGateRef` alias gave a **second name** to a host_api type that collides with the unrelated `ironclaw_host_api::ids::GateRef` (an opaque uuid `GateRecord` key, vs. turns' bounded `gate:`-prefixed routing string), so the alias was retired rather than relocated — 336 occurrences across 62 files, each verified against its import source because the two `GateRef`s are different types. All six repointed crates dropped their `ironclaw_turns` manifest dependency entirely; every symbol they used was vocabulary-only, confirming the lead sheet on that point. `RunOriginAdapter`'s validation error changed from `TurnError::InvalidRunOriginAdapter` to `Result<_, String>` (matching every other bounded ref in `host_api::turn`) with a byte-identical message pinned by a test, so the two production `e.to_string()` call sites are unchanged.
- [x] Create `contracts/ironclaw_loop_contracts` from `turns::run_profile/**` + the `LoopExit` DTO half + `CheckpointStateStorePort`; repoint `agent_loop`, `loop_host`, `hooks`, `capabilities`, `extension_host`, `host_runtime`; `agent_loop`'s deps become contracts-only with zero exceptions. **Landed with the WS1.2 PR (#6975)** (flat `crates/ironclaw_loop_contracts/`; the family directory arrives in Wave 5). `agent_loop` and `hooks` both dropped their `ironclaw_turns` manifest dependency outright, so the contracts-only rule passes with zero exceptions rather than with a waiver. Four dispositions the lead sheet did not predict, each forced by the code: (1) **The two `HostManagedLoop*Port` implementations did not move.** §6.1.4 forbids a contracts crate from implementing its own ports and §6.7.2 assigns them to `loop_host`, so `prompt.rs` and the impl half of `model.rs` stayed behind in `turns::host_managed_ports` (the module says so, and says nothing new belongs in it) awaiting the WS4 `loop_host` re-charter; the *contract* half of `model.rs` — the gateway/accountant/policy-guard traits and their request/error/outcome DTOs — moved. (2) **`turns::origin` had to move too**, to `host_api::turn`: `run_profile/runtime_context.rs` carries `Option<ProductTurnContext>` on `LoopRuntimeContext`, and the type is turn-scoped vocabulary consumed by kernel, substrates, loops, and products — exactly §6.1.1's `turn` charter. `GateResumeDisposition` followed it for the same reason (`AgentLoopDriverResumeRequest` and `ResumeTurnRequest` both carry it). (3) **`CheckpointStateStorePort` is only half-movable.** `RedactedCheckpointPayload` + its ceiling moved; the `LoopCheckpointStore` trait did not, because its methods return `TurnError` and its record carries `TurnTimestamp`, neither of which §6.1.4 assigns to this crate. The ceiling is now a literal in contracts with an equality pin against `ironclaw_processes::MAX_PROCESS_CHECKPOINT_PAYLOAD_BYTES` in `turns` (which depends on both). (4) **`LoopExit::validate` split from its DTO**: the claim vocabulary moved, the validation (policy, violation taxonomy, applier) stayed, and `LoopBlockedKind::to_blocked_reason` / `LoopFailureKind::to_sanitized_failure` / `LoopCompleted::has_durable_completion_ref` became `pub` because the kernel now calls them across a crate boundary (`to_blocked_reason`'s `Result<_, ()>` became `Option<_>` on the way out — behavior-identical, and `()` is not a public error type). Because both enums are `#[non_exhaustive]`, the kernel's exhaustive-match guards could no longer be exhaustive, so compiler-checked variant guards now live beside the enums in `loop_contracts` and the kernel's behavioral suites keep their loud `_` arms pointing at them.
- [x] Create `contracts/ironclaw_extension_contracts`: `ChannelAdapter`/`ToolAdapter`/`Extension`/entrypoint vocabulary, channel manifest-surface descriptors, auth recipe schema, memory manifest surface, `InstallationState`/`LifecyclePublicState`/`AuthAccountState`, channel-identity hooks, `PreferenceTargetCodec` + `ReplyTargetBindingRef`; close the dual import path (forbid cross-crate `pub use` of these traits). **Landed with the WS1.3 PR (#6977)** (flat `crates/ironclaw_extension_contracts/`; family directory in Wave 5), holding nine modules: `channel`, `channel_identity`, `extension`, `memory`, `package_lifecycle`, `preference_target`, `recipe`, `state`, `surface`. Five dispositions the lead sheet did not predict, each forced by the code or already true:
  1. **`ChannelAdapter`, `ToolAdapter`, and `RestrictedEgress` did not move, and cannot until WS1.4.** `host_api::product_surface` — which holds `ProductSurface` itself, unambiguously §6.1.3 material — names both in `ChannelInboundProductSurface::admit_channel_inbound_with_attachment_transfer(request, Arc<dyn ChannelAdapter>, Arc<dyn RestrictedEgress>)`. `ironclaw_host_api`'s allowlist is "no `ironclaw_*`" (pinned in `reborn_dependency_boundaries.rs`), so a type leaving `host_api` while a type staying in `host_api` names it is mechanically impossible. The direction is one-way: `product_contracts` may depend on `extension_contracts` (§6.1.3), so **WS1.4 unblocks the adapters, not the reverse** — this is an ordering finding of the §12.1c class, not a scope cut. Their conformance suite (§11.2.10) waits with them.
  2. **`package_lifecycle` moved as a forced co-mover, and §6.1.3 may want it back.** It is typed on `InstallationState`, `LifecyclePublicState`, `ChannelPresentation`, and `CapabilitySurfaceKind` — four §6.1.2 types, three of them named in this row — so the same allowlist that blocks the adapters would have blocked *those three* had it stayed. §6.1.3 assigns it to `product_contracts`; since that crate may depend on this one, re-homing it in WS1.4 costs nothing and nothing here depends on it staying. Recorded in the crate's CLAUDE.md as an interim placement rather than presented as a decision.
  3. **`AuthAccountState` is not in `host_api::state`** — it is `ironclaw_auth::account_state`, next to the engine that drives it, and its own module doc says so deliberately ("the definition lives here with the engine"). It stayed: moving the enum without `AuthAccountLastError` and `project_auth_account_state` would split a state machine from its projection, which is a domain call, not a contracts extraction. The row's parenthetical "(today `host_api::state`)" is wrong for this one of the three.
  4. **`ReplyTargetBindingRef` was already home.** It is a `bounded_ref!` in `host_api::turn` (`turn.rs:280`) — WS1.1's canonical turn vocabulary. Moving it to the extension tier would undo that consolidation and force `loop_host`, `product`, and `composition` onto `extension_contracts` for *turn* vocabulary. §6.1.2's rationale for naming it ("the product/turns types that force `telegram_extension`'s upward edges") is satisfied without it: telegram reached `ironclaw_assistant` for `PreferenceTargetCodec`, not for this.
  5. **The dual import path was worse than "a re-export".** `PreferenceTargetCodec` had three paths — `host_api::product_adapter` (which Slack used), `ironclaw_assistant` (which Telegram used, carrying a forbidden `product` dependency to do it), and `ironclaw_composition`, which re-exported *product's* re-export. `InstallationState` had a second path through `ironclaw_extension_host::state`, a facade with no external consumer whose own crate then used `crate::InstallationState` internally. All are deleted, `extension_host/src/state.rs` with them, and §11.2.4's scan pins the rule.
  6. **`hosted_mcp` joined the crate on the main merge-down, forced by a cycle.** #6930 landed `ironclaw_host_api::hosted_mcp` while WS1.3 was open, and the two modules reference each other — `hosted_mcp` names `package_lifecycle::LifecyclePackageId`, `package_lifecycle` names `hosted_mcp::RegisterHostedMcpRequest`. Legal inside one crate; once `package_lifecycle` moved, keeping `hosted_mcp` behind would have required `host_api -> extension_contracts`, which the zero-internal-dep allowlist forbids outright. Both halves now live in `extension_contracts`, which is also where §6.1.2 puts them (registration input describing what an installable extension *is*; the module doc already assigns every behavior around them to the extension host). Note the failure mode: the two sides sit in different files, so this produces **no git conflict at all** — it surfaces only as a compile error, or as pressure to weaken the `host_api` rule. `ironclaw_mcp` gained the `extension_contracts` dependency as the one consumer that did not already have it, which is the direction WS3's `mcp` row wants anyway.
  **`ironclaw_telegram_extension` dropped `ironclaw_assistant` entirely** — normal *and* dev dependency — which is what §6.1.2 promised the codec re-home would buy. It gained the boundary rule it never had (its Slack sibling, same shape, already forbade `product`), so the edge cannot come back. Also forced: `ironclaw_loop_contracts` gained an `ironclaw_extension_contracts` dependency, because `LoopRuntimeContext` carries `Option<ChannelPresentation>` and `render_presentation_hint` reads it. §8.2's contracts row sanctions it ("others: host_api/common ± extension_contracts"); §6.1.4's list predates the extension tier existing, and slot 2's allowlist was amended in place with that evidence rather than waived.
- [x] Create `contracts/ironclaw_product_contracts`: `ProductSurface`/`BoundProductSurface`/caller + descriptor types, product wire DTOs (incl. `AppEvent` from `common::event`), `package_lifecycle`/`operator_llm` vocabulary, delivery-resolution/admission/operator/lifecycle-service ports, prompt-view DTOs. **Landed with the WS1.4 PR (#6980)** (flat `crates/ironclaw_product_contracts/`; family directory in Wave 5), holding seven modules: `surface`, `inbound`, `outbound`, `projection`, `interaction_commands`, `operator_llm`, `package_lifecycle` — **plus the WS1.3 adapter half it existed to unblock**, which landed in the same PR. Seven dispositions the lead sheet did not predict, each forced by the code:
  1. **The adapter move is what forced the shape of the whole slice.** Moving `ChannelAdapter`/`ToolAdapter`/`RestrictedEgress` out of `ironclaw_host_api` is only legal once *nothing that stays in `host_api` names them* — and `product_adapter` is one connected component: `channel_adapter` names `inbound::ProductTriggerReason`, `inbound` names `channel_adapter::ChannelAttachmentRef` **and** `outbound::ProjectionCursor`, `projection` names both, `interaction_commands` names `inbound`, and `product_surface` names all of them. So `inbound`, `outbound`, `projection`, `interaction_commands`, and `product_surface` had to leave together with the adapters. That co-movement, not the `ProductSurface` trait alone, is the real content of WS1.4.
  2. **`ProductTriggerReason` moved to the extension tier, not the product tier.** It is stamped by the adapter on every `NormalizedInboundMessage`, so leaving it in `inbound` would have made `extension_contracts` depend on `product_contracts` — the forbidden direction. It sits in `channel_adapter` beside the field that carries it.
  3. **The auth-prompt view family is extension-tier, and §6.1.3's "auth/approval prompt-view DTOs" splits.** `ChannelAdapter`'s own `OutboundPart::AuthPrompt` carries `AuthPromptView`, and *both* shipped channel packages call `render_channel_auth_prompt` from `deliver` — so the auth half crosses the host↔extension membrane by an adapter signature and is now `extension_contracts::auth_prompt` (`AuthPromptView`, `AuthPromptChallengeKind`, `ConnectionPromptContext`, `PairingPromptView`, `AuthPromptContextView`). The **approval** half (`ApprovalPrompt*View`) is reached only by product and WebUI and stayed in `product_contracts::outbound`. One deliberate, recorded cost: two ~15-line display-text validators now exist in both crates, because hoisting them would have made a generic text validator part of the extension membrane's public API to serve a product-tier caller.
  4. **`external` and `egress` moved to `extension_contracts`, and §6.1.3's "external product-halves" is empty at this base.** All five `external` types — including `ProductAttachmentDescriptor`/`Kind` — are named by `ChannelAdapter`'s own cone, so there is no product half to keep; §6.1.2's "fed by" list already names both modules.
  5. **`identity` and `product_adapter_error` could not leave `host_api`.** `host_api::user_identity` names `AdapterInstallationId`, and `host_api::product_adapter::auth` (which stays until WS1's sealed-evidence row moves it) names `ProductAdapterError`. `host_api` may hold no internal dependency, so the only placement that serves both contracts tiers is below both. §6.1.3's "fed by" naming `product_adapter_error` is not achievable while `auth` stays.
  6. **`package_lifecycle` came back to `product_contracts`, as §6.1.3 says and WS1.3 anticipated.** The § text names it under product wire DTO homes and §6.1.2's Owns list does not; the code agrees, since `LifecycleProductAction`/`LifecycleProductResponse` are the vocabulary of `LifecycleProductService`, which §6.1.3 also assigns here. Zero cost, because this crate may depend on `extension_contracts`, so the four §6.1.2 types it is typed on stay reachable. *Merge-down note: main's #6930 adds `host_api::hosted_mcp`, which is mutually referencing with `package_lifecycle` (`hosted_mcp` needs `LifecyclePackageId`; `LifecycleProductAction` gains an `ExtensionRegisterHostedMcp { request: RegisterHostedMcpRequest }` variant). `hosted_mcp` is §6.1.2 material by its own module doc ("the extension host owns endpoint canonicalization, SSRF checks, identity derivation, … and durable admission") and by its consumers (9 of 14 are `extension_host`/`extensions`/`mcp`/`auth`), so the clean resolution is `hosted_mcp` → `extension_contracts` with `LifecyclePackageId` following it, and `product_contracts::package_lifecycle` importing that one bounded-string id from below. Putting the pair in `product_contracts` instead would give `ironclaw_mcp` a product-tier edge, which is exactly what §6.1.2 exists to prevent. **This merge-down happened during WS1.4's review and the ruling held**: the parent branch relocated `hosted_mcp` into `extension_contracts`, and WS1.4 split the coupling as recorded — `LifecyclePackageId` (with `LifecycleBlockerRef`, which shares its bounded-string template) moved to a new `extension_contracts::lifecycle_id` module, the projections stayed product-tier, and `package_lifecycle` imports the id from below.*
  7. **`AppEvent` did not come, because it is dead.** §6.1.3 assigns the "43-variant `AppEvent` wire enum" here on the premise that a transport streams it to a client. Re-measured on this base: `crates/ironclaw_common/src/event.rs` is **1,234 lines with zero consumers outside `ironclaw_common` itself** — the only other workspace reference is an architecture test asserting the Reborn OpenAI-compatible routes must *not* stream it. Moving it would seed a new contracts crate with dead weight and a size-ceiling problem (§11.2.3). It stays in `common` and is flagged for deletion on the "Narrow `ironclaw_common`" row below, which also owns `event.rs`. This is an inventory correction, not a scope cut.
  **What it bought:** `ChannelAdapter`, `ToolAdapter`, and `RestrictedEgress` are in `ironclaw_extension_contracts` where §6.1.2 assigns them, and the §11.2.10 channel-adapter conformance suite moved with them (both channel packages now run it from its owner). Four re-export chains that gave one trait two import paths are deleted — `ChannelAdapter` through `ironclaw_assistant` *and* through `ironclaw_composition`, `OutboundDeliverySink`/`ProtocolHttpEgress` through `ironclaw_assistant`, and `ProjectionStream`/`ChannelInboundProductSurface` through `ironclaw_assistant` — each pinned by the new §11.2.4 scan. **Deferred by design, not missed:** §6.1.3's product-side *ports* (delivery resolution, admission, the operator service set, `LifecycleProductService`, `AccountConnectionStatusSource`, `ChannelConfigProductService`) and the command/view/capability descriptor types are sourced from `ironclaw_assistant`, not from `host_api`, and WS2's first row and WS6's `operator` row own them by name — moving a port definition without repointing its implementor buys no dependency-edge removal. The WS1.4 PR body carries a per-port movability map (11 of 15 movable with import repointing only; `ProductOutboundTargetResolver`, `ProductCommandAdmissionService`, and `LifecycleProductService` blocked on `ironclaw_outbound`/`ironclaw_turns` types those rows must move first).
- [x] Consolidate sealed evidence minting: channel/webhook verified-inbound constructors into `extension_contracts` (mintable only by the generic verifier), bearer/session evidence into `host_api`; delete the `host-auth-mint` cargo feature everywhere; port the existing ingress/auth contract tests and add a refute-test that adapters/product cannot mint. **(security-sensitive — PROPOSAL §12.1a)** **Landed with the WS1.5 PR (#6981).** The channel/webhook mint family is `ironclaw_extension_contracts::verified_inbound` (§6.1.2); the bearer/session family stays in `ironclaw_host_api::product_adapter::auth` (§6.1.1); the `host-auth-mint` feature is gone from all five manifests that declared or enabled it and from the one CI recipe that selected it. Enforcement is `reborn_sealed_evidence_mint_ratchet` (10 tests, §11.2.5). Six findings, each measured on this base rather than taken from the row:
  1. **The feature was not a seal, and the measurement is the reason this row mattered.** Cargo unifies features across the packages selected in one invocation, so `ironclaw_webui`'s `ironclaw_assistant = { features = ["host-auth-mint"] }` (→ `ironclaw_turns/host-auth-mint` → `ironclaw_host_api/host-auth-mint`) compiled `ironclaw_host_api` **once, with the gate on**, for every other crate in the same build. Proven before touching anything: a probe test in `ironclaw_agent_loop` — whose manifest names `ironclaw_host_api` with *no* features — called `mark_bearer_token_verified("attacker")` and minted a verified bearer claim; it failed to compile under `cargo test -p ironclaw_agent_loop` and **passed** under `cargo test -p ironclaw_agent_loop -p ironclaw_webui`. Every workspace build (`cargo test`, `cargo check --all-targets --all-features`, CI) is the second case. So this row is a real tightening, not a relocation: the replacement is the repo's existing witness-token idiom (`host_api::authorized`), which no other crate's manifest can switch on.
  2. **Two grants, not one, and the seam is split across two crates by force.** `HostAuthenticationGrant` ← `HostProtocolAuthenticator` (sole implementor `ironclaw_webui`, on the *module-private* `AuthLayerState`, minted a few lines after `authenticate()` returns `Some`); `VerifiedInboundGrant` ← `ChannelIngressVerifier` (sole implementor `ironclaw_extension_host`, on `VerifiedEvidenceMint` — the value that *is* the recipe the router just executed, so grant and claim derive from one verification). One grant would let either minter forge the other's claim shape. Both grants and both traits had to stay in `host_api`: the verified variant is private to its module, so `extension_contracts` reaches it through the grant-gated `ProtocolAuthEvidence::seal_verified_inbound`, and a grant defined in `extension_contracts` could not be constructed by a `host_api` method. **The evidence *type* therefore did not move** — the row says "bearer/session *evidence* into `host_api`", and §6.1.1 already owns the type; what moved is the channel/webhook *constructors*. WS1.4's disposition 5 ("`product_adapter::auth` stays until WS1's sealed-evidence row moves it") is resolved this way: the module stays, its channel mint family leaves.
  3. **Five of the eight mint functions had zero callers.** Only `mark_bearer_token_verified_for_tenant` (webui), `mark_request_signature_verified`, and `mark_shared_secret_header_verified` (both `extension_host`) were ever called; `mark_bearer_token_verified`, `mark_session_verified`, `mark_session_verified_for_tenant`, `mark_request_signature_verified_for_tenant`, and `mark_shared_secret_header_verified_for_tenant` were dead public mint surface. Kept, because §6.1.2 names the family and the row is a move, and because under the witness they are now reachable only by the two sanctioned minters — but recorded on the WS8 follow-ups below as a deletion candidate, since dead mint surface is pure attack surface.
  4. **Four re-export chains, not one.** `host_api::product_adapter` re-exported all eight from `auth`; `ironclaw_assistant` re-exported all eight at its crate root **and again** from `product::auth` (two paths out of one crate — the second is how `extension_host` reached them); and `ironclaw_composition` re-exported `mark_bearer_token_verified_for_tenant` with **zero consumers**, an app-layer crate widening a security seam it did not use. All four deleted, pinned by the ratchet's `no_crate_re_exports_a_mint_function_it_does_not_own`; the composition pub-use snapshot lost exactly that one line.
  5. **Two feature opt-ins were stale, not load-bearing.** `ironclaw_turns`'s `host-auth-mint = ["ironclaw_host_api/host-auth-mint"]` had zero uses in `turns` — it existed only so `ironclaw_assistant` could chain through it. `ironclaw_telegram_extension`'s dev-dep opt-in was stale too: the crate names no mint function and no `ProtocolAuthEvidence` at all, and its manifest comment attributed the need to `payload::tests`, which lives in `ironclaw_telegram_v2_adapter`. Both deleted with the comment corrected rather than carried.
  6. **Tests moved to `tests/`, following the `Authorized` precedent, and the ratchet is what forced it.** The first ratchet run failed on the seal's *own* inline `#[cfg(test)]` doubles in `host_api` and `extension_contracts` — the same collision `authorized.rs` records ("tests live in `tests/authorized_seal.rs` … a test double under `tests/` is not inventoried"). Rather than carve `#[cfg(test)]` out of the scanner (which would make an inline test module a hiding place for a grant source), the doubles moved: `host_api/tests/protocol_auth_evidence_seal.rs` (5) and `extension_contracts/tests/verified_inbound_seal.rs` (4). The four adapter/integration call sites that previously *minted* in tests (`slack_extension::payload` ×2, `telegram_v2_adapter::payload` ×2, `tests/integration/extension_delivery.rs` ×3) now use `ProtocolAuthEvidence::test_verified` — value-identical, since `test_verified` and the old `mark_*` both delegate to `host_verified`. No surviving assertion was edited. The root manifest's `ironclaw_host_api` dev-dep gained an explicit `features = ["test-support"]` rather than inheriting it through a sibling's unification — the same masking class this row exists to remove.

The two `→ resources` survivors are the only lane exceptions left and both are blocked on the same thing — the `ResourceGovernor` authority port, not vocabulary. See the WS3 `mcp` row. (Authored against 13 as "now 11"; recomputed as `len()` of the **merged** list when this PR merged `main` down, per the union rule on the §11.2.2 row.)* ✎ *2026-08-04 (WS3, #7067): **4 → 2**. Both `→ resources` lane rows are deleted and the baseline lowered in the same change. The port that unblocked them is `ironclaw_host_api::resource::RuntimeResourceBudget` (reserve/reconcile/release only), implemented in the kernel over any `ResourceGovernor` as `ironclaw_resources::GovernorRuntimeBudget` — dependency inversion, not the relocation §8.3's amendment rules out. Both lanes dropped the production dependency; `ironclaw_resources` stays a dev-dependency in each so the lane suites keep driving the port over the real governor. The register on that branch is `host_runtime → extension_support` and `conversations → turns`.*
  - **The wave's other milestone clauses did land**: all three contracts crates exist (`ironclaw_loop_contracts`, `ironclaw_extension_contracts`, `ironclaw_product_contracts`), `agent_loop` passes contracts-only with **zero** exceptions, and the channel/product crates can now compile against contracts (the flips are Wave 2 by design).
  - **Evidence-mint is sealed, and the row understated what it bought.** WS1.5's measurement is the finding to carry forward: the `host-auth-mint` cargo feature was **never a seal** — Cargo unifies features across the packages selected in one invocation, so every workspace-wide build (`cargo test`, `cargo check --all-features`, CI) compiled `ironclaw_host_api` with the gate **on** for every crate. Proven with a probe test that failed to compile alone and passed under `-p ironclaw_agent_loop -p ironclaw_webui`. The replacement is the witness-token idiom, which no manifest can switch on. Treat "a cargo feature gates this" as an unproven claim until measured the same way.

## WS2 — Extensions family (re-layers `extension_host` to `loops`; packages colocated)

> ✎ **Heading corrected 2026-08-04 — the milestone was MIS-SEQUENCED, not structurally forbidden.** The heading read *"kills `extension_host→product`"* and promised it *at this row*, which is what made every slot re-derive a correction. The edge **does** die — it just cannot die before the port residue empties.
>
> ⚠ **This note itself was wrong when first written earlier the same day, and the replacement is quoted rather than silently overwritten** (a doc-truth audit caught it; this is the sixth mis-statement of this seam, and it landed inside the PR whose purpose was finally stating it right). **Superseded text:** *"which is not reachable and never was: a caller must name product's **frozen** command/view/capability constants to reach the surface, and §6.1.3 keeps those in product deliberately … the edge becomes legal-by-layer or is replaced by contracts-declared ports; it does not have to vanish."* Wrong twice:
>
> - **The frozen-constants argument belongs to §12.11 D-B's crates, not this one.** It is what makes `webui → product` and `openai_compat`/`extension_manager → product` permanent, and borrowing it here was a category error. Measured on this branch: `ironclaw_extension_host`'s production code names **zero** of the **136** `*_COMMAND`/`*_VIEW`/`*_CAPABILITY` constants `ironclaw_assistant` defines. It names ports, DTOs, concrete drivers, and `adapter_registry::PRODUCT_ADAPTER_HOST_API_ID` — nothing from the frozen inventory, and nothing has since WS2.4 moved the product face to `extension_manager`.
> - **"It does not have to vanish" is backwards.** After the flip the crate is `loops`, and `loops → products` is illegal, so the edge **must** vanish — there is no "legal-by-layer" outcome available to it. That is what the re-layer row below already says ("delete the manifest edge and flip the layer line in one change") and what `the_extension_host_manifest_names_product_only_while_a_residue_needs_it` mechanically enforces: the manifest dependency is asserted to exist **exactly while** the port residue is non-empty, so the last residue row and the manifest edge have to go in the same change, in either direction. That biconditional is the mechanism; it makes the edge's death a scheduled event, not an optional one.
>
> So the wave's goal is the **layer flip**, and the edge dies *with* it. Row 1 of this section and PLAN's Wave 2 note both say the milestone overstated *this row*; neither says the edge survives.

- [x] Flip `extension_host`'s implemented ports to `product_contracts`/`extension_contracts` definitions (delivery resolver, reply-context source, admission, pairing sources, account-status) — **must land before the layer flip** (ordering constraint, PROPOSAL §12.1c). **Landed with the WS2.1 PR.** All five named families moved, plus six more §6.1.3 names the row did not list — **eleven port declarations relocated, nine of them implemented by `extension_host`** (the nine are pinned as `INVERTED_PORTS` in the new `reborn_extension_host_port_inversion.rs`; `AdminUserService` and `RebornOperatorToolCatalog` are the two `extension_host` only *consumes*, implemented in composition): `ChannelDeliveryResolver`+`ResolvedChannelDelivery`, `DeliveryReplyContextSource`, `CommandActorRoleResolver` (+`ProductCommandContext`, `ProductActionId`, `ActionFingerprintKey`, the bounded product tokens), `ApprovalPromptContextSource`+`BlockedAuthPromptSource`(+`BlockedAuthPromptRequest`), `AccountConnectionStatusSource` (+the account-setup descriptor/notice/error DTOs), `ChannelConfigProductService`, `RebornViewProvider`(+descriptor/query/page), `LifecycleProductService`(+its two contexts), `AdminUserService`(+records), `RebornOperatorToolCatalog`(+info). Four dispositions the lead sheet did not predict:
  1. **The row's headline effect is not reachable in this slot, and the reason is structural.** The wave milestone says the `extension_host → product` edge dies here. It does not: **62 product symbols across 35 production files survive** (down from 146 across 46). Every survivor is owned by a *later* WS2 row, and the mapping is exact — `channel_host.rs` constructs product's concrete assembly (`DefaultProductSurface`, `DefaultInboundTurnService`, `RebornFilesystemIdempotencyLedger`, `StaticProductInstallationResolver`, `DirectConversationCommandAdmission::new`, `ProductConversationBindingService::new`); `admin_configuration*.rs`/`operator_config_capability.rs`/`skill_auto_activate_capability.rs`/`available_extensions.rs`/`product_lifecycle.rs`/`webui_extension_credentials.rs` are the `extension_manager` split inventory; `channel_lifecycle.rs`/`available_extensions.rs`/`host_api_contracts.rs` reach `product::adapter_registry`, which §6.9.1 sheds to `extension_contracts`/`extension_registry`; `skill_learning.rs` and `channel_triggered_delivery.rs` are named strays. **The edge dies at the end of WS2, not at this row** — which is exactly what §12.1c's ordering says ("port inversions land *before* the layer flip"), and PLAN's per-row milestone overstated it.
  2. **Six ports could not move, all for one mechanical reason.** `product_contracts`' allowlist is `host_api` + `extension_contracts` and nothing else internal, so a port whose signature names `ironclaw_auth`, `ironclaw_threads`, `ironclaw_turns`, or `ironclaw_conversations` cannot be declared there: `AuthChallengeProvider`, `ChannelConnectionService`, `ExtensionCredentialSetupService` (auth credential vocabulary), and `ConversationBindingService`, `ProductActorUserResolver`, `ProductConversationSubjectRouteResolver` (they error with `ProductSurfaceFailure`, which carries `ironclaw_turns::TurnError`). The residue is enumerated with per-entry reasons and held **shrink-only** by the new `reborn_extension_host_port_inversion.rs`; the durable finding is that **`ProductSurfaceFailure` is the linchpin** — `extension_host` uses product's *internal workflow error type* as its own lifecycle error vocabulary in 19 production files, and no port that errors with it can be inverted while the type lives in `ironclaw_assistant`. ✎ **Corrected 2026-08-01 (was: "until it is either narrowed off `ironclaw_turns` or replaced by an extension-host-owned error"):** both of those are insufficient — the enforced allowlist admits only `host_api` and `extension_contracts`, never product and never the extension host — so the fix is a port-facing error defined in an allowed crate and mapped to `ProductSurfaceFailure` inside product. See the dedicated row below.
  3. **`ProductCommandAdmissionService` is a §6.1.3-vs-§6.9.1 conflict, recorded not decided.** §6.1.3 assigns "the `ProductCommandAdmissionService` shape" to contracts; its `admit` takes `&ProductCommand`, and §6.9.1 keeps the command grammar with product's frozen inventory. Left in `ironclaw_assistant`. ✅ **[decision] RESOLVED 2026-08-02 (delegated authority — PROPOSAL §12.11 D-D): it stays in `ironclaw_assistant`, and §6.1.3's Owns list drops it.** §6.9.1 wins because the port has **one consumer crate**: three implementors (`RejectingProductCommandAdmissionService` `command_dispatch.rs:33`, `DirectConversationCommandAdmission` `command_admission.rs:28`, plus a test double) and one call site (`workflow.rs:1186`), **all inside `ironclaw_assistant`** — `grep -rl ProductCommandAdmissionService crates/` returns 7 files, every one of them product's. `ironclaw_extension_host` constructs `DirectConversationCommandAdmission` (`channel_host.rs:800`) but never names the trait; the `Arc<Concrete> → Arc<dyn Trait>` coercion does not bring it into scope. So the port carries no dependency edge, and `families/contracts.md:21`'s admission test ("two or more consumers that must not import one another") is failed on its own terms. **Two doc refutations:** the WS1.4 movability map (this row, `PROPOSAL §6.1.3` as-built note 4) says this port is "blocked on `ironclaw_outbound`/`ironclaw_turns` types" — **false**; its signature names neither (`ProductCommandContext` and `ProductSurfaceError` are already in `product_contracts`, as is `ProductRejection`), and its sole blocker is `ProductCommand` itself, a §6.9.1 grammar question. The same map's `LifecycleProductService` entry is **stale** — it moved (`product_contracts/src/lifecycle_service.rs:42`). Only `ProductOutboundTargetResolver`'s entry survives intact. Note the interaction: once D-A's factory port lands, the construction at `channel_host.rs:800` moves into product's factory implementation, so extension_host stops touching this family at all.
  4. **`extension_host` carries no `LAYER_MATRIX_EXCEPTIONS` entry, and never did.** The wave's "exception entries it carries fall" expectation is wrong at the mechanism level: `extension_host` and `product` are both `products`-layer, so the edge is *legal* by the matrix and invisible to it. Re-verified against this PR's base: the register holds 13 and none of the 13 names `extension_host`, `product`, or `product_contracts`. **The count stays 13.** What this PR removes instead is 84 facade symbols and nine mis-placed port declarations, pinned by a new architecture test rather than by the exception register.
  ✎ **Coverage-gate note for every later Wave 2/3 slot (added 2026-08-01 by WS2.1).** A move-shaped PR reliably fails `reborn_changed_coverage.py` on its first CI run, and the failure has three distinct shapes that need three different answers. Budget one extra CI cycle for it. (1) **A relocated declaration-only module reports "absent from coverage or contain no DA records"** — rustc emits no LCOV source record for a file of pure traits and DTOs. The answer is a contract test in the module (object safety, argument pass-through, the absence-without-error shape), not a waiver; WS2.1 closed five modules this way. (2) **A file reports "contributed no instrumented lines"** when the only lines the diff added are *type positions* — a struct field, a function parameter, or a return type whose path was repointed. Those carry no LLVM coverage region and can never be covered; they are the exemption class WS1.1/WS1.4 established, filed under #6963. (3) **Genuine holes hide behind the noise.** Relocating a symbol re-writes the line that names it, which drags previously-uncovered *error* paths into the diff: WS2.1 found two fail-closed seams that way (`AccountConnectionStatusSource::connected`'s sanitization, and the lifecycle output-decode mapping) and one dead branch arm. Those are the real find, so read the uncovered-lines list before reaching for the manifest. **Two mechanical tips that shrink all three:** prefer a `use` import to an inline fully-qualified path — a `use` line is uninstrumentable by construction, and it also keeps the rest of the statement textually unchanged, so it stays diff context; and extract an inline `map_err` closure into a named function when the repoint re-wraps it, which makes the mapping testable instead of exemptible.
  ✎ **Amended 2026-08-02 (Wave 2 truth audit) — the gate changed under this note twice after it was written, and both changes cut directly across its advice.** Kept rather than rewritten because its three failure shapes are still real; read the note *with* these two.
  1. **The gate now excludes changed lines whose pre-image was already uncovered at the base commit** (`scripts/ci/reborn_changed_coverage.py`). Under `--unified=0` the i-th added line pairs with the i-th removed line, a second `git diff` (with `-C`, deliberately separate so copy detection can never shrink the denominator) supplies the pre-image, and a line is dropped only on a **positive observation** that its pre-image was uncovered at base — no pre-image, no base `DA` record, or a pre-image that *was* covered all keep the line, so filling a hole is never punished. **This is shape (2)'s answer, automated**: a repointed type-position line that was uncovered before is now removed by the rule rather than by a manifest entry, and reaching for an exemption first is now the wrong move. Base lcov is fetched from the base SHA's `reborn-tests.yml` run artifact and is available for roughly **17 of 30** consecutive `main` commits (the misses are runs cancelled by `concurrency.cancel-in-progress`), so budget for the fallback: it is **genuinely fail-closed and loud** — every acquisition failure raises `BaseCoverageUnavailable`, the exclusion is skipped entirely, *every* changed line counts, and the run prints "Base coverage: NOT APPLIED … falling back to the strict denominator". **The one consequence to know:** the subtraction is applied to the candidate set, not the measured set, so a PR whose every changed line is excluded passes on a **zero denominator** — a pure move can be green having measured nothing. That is deliberate (it is what stops the empty-denominator assertion firing on lines the rule legitimately removed), but it means "the coverage gate passed" is not evidence a move PR was tested.
  2. **⚠ The changed-coverage gate does not run on an ordinary pull request any more.** #6952 scoped Reborn PR tests by affected area: `coverage-report` is gated on `coverage_mode == "full"`, and `FULL_EVENTS` is `{merge_group, push, workflow_call, workflow_dispatch, schedule}` — **`pull_request` is not in it**. A PR whose changed paths all map to known packages runs `mode = "selected"`, `coverage_mode = "none"`, and the whole job is skipped; it escalates to full only on an empty diff, an unmapped path, or a package outside the canonical set. Combined with #6978 (dispatch runs fail the roll-up structurally) and the fact that `reborn-tests.yml` attaches only to PRs targeting `main`, **a stacked slice gets its changed-coverage verdict for the first time in the merge queue.** Plan a slice's coverage work from a local `--base`/`--head` run or a dispatch, not from PR CI. ✎ **Corrected 2026-08-03 (WS3, #7064) — this row named the wrong constant and drew the wrong conclusion from it; the corrected version is the reason #7064's floor edit could not be verified on its own PR.** The sentence removed read: *"One sharp edge in the same mechanism: `FULL_PR_PATHS` lists `tests/integration/coverage-floor.toml` but **not** `changed-coverage-exemptions.toml`, so a PR that only edits the exemption manifest does not run the gate that consumes it."* **There is no `FULL_PR_PATHS` in this repo** — 0 occurrences on this branch *and* on live `main` fetched through the GitHub contents API (counted in Python, not grep, to rule out regex quirks); the module-level constants in `scripts/ci/reborn_pr_test_plan.py` are `ROOT`, `MAX_PR_CRATE_BUCKETS`, `FULL_EVENTS`, `IGNORED_PREFIXES`, `DEDICATED_WORKFLOW_PREFIXES`, `QA_HARNESS_PREFIXES`, `CHANGED_COVERAGE_MANIFEST`, `PR_STATIC_CONTROL_PATHS`, `PR_STATIC_CONTROL_PREFIXES`, `BUCKET_WEIGHTS`. **The set that really exists is `PR_STATIC_CONTROL_PATHS` (`:36`), it contains BOTH `coverage-floor.toml` (`:43`) and `coverage-exemptions.toml` (`:42`), and its consuming branch (`:353`) DE-escalates rather than escalates** — it appends *"static CI or workspace-policy checks own: {path}"* and `continue`s, so the path contributes no package and the plan stays `selected` / `coverage_mode: none`. So the old sentence was wrong twice: the mechanism is misnamed, and its implied contrast (floor escalates, exemption manifest does not) is backwards — **measured, neither escalates.** Three probes, each a single real path in a real file on disk (never process substitution, and never mixed with a `docs/` path — `docs/` is in `IGNORED_PREFIXES`, so folding one into a probe can mask what is being measured): `coverage-floor.toml` → `coverage_mode: none` (*"static CI or workspace-policy checks own"*); `changed-coverage-exemptions.toml` → `coverage_mode: none` (*"changed-coverage policy is statically validated"* — its own dedicated arm, not an omission from a set); `coverage-exemptions.toml` → `coverage_mode: none`. **The old sentence's bottom line — a PR editing only the exemption manifest does not run the gate that consumes it — happens to be true, but by a different mechanism, and it is equally true of the floor file, which is the part that mattered here.** **Decisive confirmation independent of any code reading: on #7064 itself, which edits `coverage-floor.toml`, the `Reborn integration-tier coverage report` check is `skipping`** ([run 30857632335](https://github.com/nearai/ironclaw/actions/runs/30857632335), job 91836054560) — it did not run on the PR, and the ratchet verdict came only from a `workflow_dispatch` run. **So editing a coverage floor buys a PR no ratchet verdict: the floor edit and the gate that checks it sit on opposite sides of the `pull_request` gap.** **The load-bearing generalisation, and it is stronger than this bullet's opening states — NO PR-TRIGGERED LANE PRODUCES A COVERAGE VERDICT FOR ANY CHANGED PATH.** Not for the floor file, not for the exemption manifests, not for ordinary crate source. This is structural, not a sample: `_full_plan()` — the *only* producer of `coverage_mode: "full"` — has exactly **two call sites**, both in the first four lines of `build_plan()` (`:315` `if event in FULL_EVENTS`, `:317` `if event != "pull_request"`). On `event == "pull_request"` both are false by construction, so **`_full_plan()` is unreachable and `coverage_mode` can only be `"none"`.** ✎ **This also corrects this bullet's own opening claim** that a PR *"escalates to full only on an empty diff, an unmapped path, or a package outside the canonical set"* — measured, those arms do not escalate, they **`raise` and fail the planner**: an unmapped path exits 1 with *"unclassified pull-request path: …"*, and an empty diff exits 1 with *"empty pull-request diff cannot be classified; refusing to launch an unbounded PR matrix"*. They abort the run; they never buy coverage. Probes on `--event pull_request`, single real paths: ordinary crate source → `selected` / **none**; shared integration support → `selected` / **none**; workspace `Cargo.toml` → **none**; WebUI frontend → **none**. **Consequence for every remaining Wave 3 lane: the only ways to get a coverage verdict on a slice before the merge queue are (a) a `workflow_dispatch` run of `reborn-tests.yml` — judged by the per-job tally, never the roll-up (#6978) — or (b) a local replay, either `scripts/ci/reborn-local-coverage-ratchet.sh` with `--base`/`--head`, or the cheapest and most exact option: download the last merged `reborn-integration-coverage-merged` artifact and run `scripts/ci/reborn-coverage-ratchet.sh <lcov> <exemptions> <floor>` over it, which reproduces the queue verdict byte-for-byte.** Treat a green PR page as carrying **no** coverage information whatsoever.
- [x] **Give the product-side ports a contract-nameable error — the linchpin slice, added 2026-08-01 by WS2.1 review triage, corrected the same day.** ✎ **Closed by WS2.2 — the resolution and its four dispositions are the row directly below; this row is kept for the ask it stated and the allowlist evidence behind it.** Row 1's disposition 2 named this as the durable finding but left it without a row of its own. ✎ **The resolution it stated — "narrowed off `ironclaw_turns` or replaced by an extension-host-owned error" — is wrong, and review caught it: neither is sufficient.** `product_contracts`' allowlist is enforced as a *whitelist* of exactly `{ironclaw_product_contracts, ironclaw_extension_contracts, ironclaw_host_api}` (`reborn_dependency_boundaries.rs:362-374`, whose own comment reads "**Never product, never operator, never the extension host**"). `ProductSurfaceFailure` is defined in `ironclaw_assistant` (`crates/ironclaw_assistant/src/error.rs:46`), so a port erroring with it is undeclarable in contracts **whatever it carries** — narrowing it off `ironclaw_turns::TurnError` (`error.rs:10,90,112`) removes one obstacle but not the one that binds, and an `extension_host`-owned error is barred by the same list. **The actual work:** define the port-facing error in an allowed crate — `host_api` (the neutral choice) or `extension_contracts` — and map it to `ProductSurfaceFailure` inside `ironclaw_assistant`, where the workflow type stays. Narrowing off `ironclaw_turns` is then a *sub-goal*, not the goal: it is what lets the neutral error be defined without dragging turn vocabulary into contracts. Payload: unblock the six ports row 1 could not move — `AuthChallengeProvider`, `ChannelConnectionService`, `ExtensionCredentialSetupService` (blocked instead by `ironclaw_auth` credential vocabulary, which needs the same treatment) and `ConversationBindingService`, `ProductActorUserResolver`, `ProductConversationSubjectRouteResolver` (blocked by the error) — and shrink the `reborn_extension_host_port_inversion.rs` residue baseline accordingly. It also has to reckon with the same type being `extension_host`'s own lifecycle error vocabulary across 19 production files, which the mapping layer has to absorb rather than duplicate.
- [x] **Resolve the `ProductSurfaceFailure` linchpin** — WS2.1's durable finding, owned by the row directly above. **Landed with the WS2.2 PR.** `extension_host` no longer depends on `ironclaw_assistant` for its lifecycle error vocabulary: the boundary half is `ironclaw_product_contracts::error::ProductOperationFailure` (six variants, every payload a plain `String` or nothing), product keeps `ProductSurfaceFailure` and absorbs it with a total `From`, and PROPOSAL §6.1.3 carries the dated ownership amendment with the alternatives it beat. Four dispositions:
  1. **The blocker was never the whole enum — it was two variants, and `extension_host` uses neither.** WS2.1 recorded that `ProductSurfaceFailure` "carries `ironclaw_turns::TurnError`" and so could not follow the ports into contracts. Measured on that base with `#[cfg(test)]` stripped, `extension_host` constructs exactly **six** variants across 150 production sites — `InvalidBindingRequest` (99), `Transient` (39), `ProviderInstanceNotConfigured` (5), `BindingResolutionFailed` (5), `BindingAccessDenied` (1), `UnsupportedActionKind` (1) — **all plain-`String` or unit, none of them the kernel-typed ones**. Its `TurnResumeRejected`/`TurnSubmissionRejected` uses, which the raw grep shows, are test-only. So the vocabulary the extension host needed was always declarable in contracts; what was undeclarable was the *superset*, and product is the only crate that produces or reads that.
  2. **Narrowing the type instead of splitting it would have been lossy, in an auth path.** The rejected alternative — replace the `TurnError` payload with the neutral projection product already computes — fails on live code: `auth_continuation.rs` matches all eight `TurnErrorCategory` values structurally in `surface_error_kind` and `auth_error_for_continuation_dispatch`, and distinguishes `CapacityExceeded` from `AdmissionRejected`, which `surface_rejection_kind` collapses into one; `map_auth_resume_error` constructs by matching `TurnError::{InvalidTransition, LeaseMismatch}`, which the category projection cannot express at all. Making it lossless requires moving `TurnError`/`TurnErrorCategory` to `host_api` — §6.1.1's turn-vocabulary consolidation, a different row, and a widening of what contracts may name.
  3. **The residue shrinks 6 → 5, and two of the remaining reasons were wrong.** `ProductConversationSubjectRouteResolver` had no blocker besides the error and was inverted (with `ProductConversationRouteKey` and its request type, into `product_contracts::subject_route`). The other two are **not** unblocked, and the WS2.1 reasons that named the error are now corrected in place: `ConversationBindingService` is blocked by `ResolveBindingRequest`/`ResolvedBinding`, which sit in product beside the route-kind grammar that derives them; `ProductActorUserResolver` by `ResolvedProductActorUser`'s `ironclaw_conversations::ExternalActorBindingEpoch`. Both implementations live in the files the `channel_host.rs` decision below owns.
  4. **A trait-shaped gate could not have caught this, so the scan grew a second half.** `reborn_extension_host_port_inversion.rs` now also freezes, exact-match and shrink-only, the `extension_host` production files still naming product's workflow error — **19 → 2**, exactly the two implementing the still-product-declared ports — and pins that the contracts error names none of `TurnError`/`ironclaw_turns`/`ironclaw_auth`/`ironclaw_threads`/`ironclaw_conversations`/`ironclaw_assistant`. Both halves were verified by negative probe (a code reference trips it, a comment does not, a stale row trips it, re-adding `TurnError` trips it). Enumerating gates: the extension-specificity allowlist **shrinks 130 → 129** — the moved route key carried a vendor example in its doc, which a contracts crate may not hold at all, so it was rewritten generically and the `ironclaw_assistant` carve-out deleted rather than repointed.
- [x] **The four WS2.1 follow-ups — three typed-signature corrections and one status-code flip — independent of the linchpin row above, and of each other.** **Landed with the WS2 strays-and-follow-ups PR (2026-08-02).** ✎ *Split out of that row 2026-08-01 after review pointed out that bundling them behind the blocker would hold independent work hostage; the original grouping was scheduling convenience ("the slice is already in these files"), not a dependency. Re-titled the same day: the first three are type changes, the fourth is a behavior change, and "signatures" covered only the first three.* All four were **relocated verbatim** by WS2.1 under PLAN operating principle 2 (no semantic change in a move PR) — that is their shared provenance, not a shared fix. The first three need a typed replacement plus a caller-level test at the seam named; the fourth changes a live HTTP status and is sized separately below. None of the four depends on the linchpin row: every replacement type already sits in a crate `product_contracts` may name.
  - ~~`product_contracts::prompt_source::BlockedAuthPromptRequest.gate_ref: &str` → `&TurnGateRef`~~ **done.** All three producers (`run_delivery/triggered.rs`, `run_delivery/observer.rs`, `projection/turn_events.rs`) already held `state.gate_ref` as an `Option<TurnGateRef>` and were calling `.as_str()` to fit the field, so the change *removed* three conversions rather than adding any. Caller-level pin: `prompt_projection_contract.rs` now asserts the rendered view's `auth_request_ref` **is** the request's gate ref, so the typed field is provably the one that lands (it already captured the ref reaching `challenge_for_gate`). `AuthChallengeProvider::challenge_for_gate` keeps its `&str` — it is a different port, named by no row here.

The projection now happens once where the durable record's bare-string key is read, the three `.as_str()`s are gone, and the product-side consumer mints an `ExtensionId` from the package id (absent ⇒ no error, since a key that is not extension vocabulary could never have matched). Verified against the merged tree before ticking — `channel_host.rs` is still in `ironclaw_extension_host` (it was not moved to composition, which is the half option (a) would have executed), `channel_outbound_targets.rs` and `channel_triggered_delivery.rs` still hold their `Arc<GenericChannelHostAssembly>`, and `ChannelWorkflowStateFactory` is still the injected precedent the ruling reasoned from. **The follow-on implementation — building the factory port — is not this row and is not done**; it is one of the two blockers itemised on the re-layer row below, which stays open and carries the measurement. Ticking the decision does not tick the build. **`channel_host.rs` stays in `ironclaw_extension_host`; the product-stack construction inverts behind a factory port declared in `ironclaw_product_contracts` and implemented in `ironclaw_assistant`, supplied through the `GenericChannelHostDeps` bundle composition already builds.** Option (a) — move to composition — is rejected: the file is not boot wiring but a supervised runtime reconciler (`GenericChannelHostAssembly::start` at `:454` spawns a `tokio` watch loop over `SnapshotWatch`; `reconcile` at `:622` diffs per extension, drains, and swaps registry entries race-safely; `Drop` at `:1127` aborts it), and its move-closure is three more files (`channel_outbound_targets.rs:78` and `channel_triggered_delivery.rs:48` both hold `Arc<GenericChannelHostAssembly>`, and `extension_host → composition` is illegal at every layer) — 2,225 production + 5,469 test lines of behaviour into the family whose charter is "nothing but wiring". Option (b)'s stated cost is refuted: the factory-port pattern **already exists in this very file** (`ChannelWorkflowStateFactory`, `:152`, doc at `:149` "Injected so this module names no concrete backend"), its implementation is already constructed by composition (`extension_host_assembly.rs:475`), and the "two or more consumers" objection miscounts — for an inversion port the two consumers are the declaring caller and the implementing owner, which is what `families/contracts.md:21` says. **`channel_triggered_delivery.rs` is covered by the same ruling** (it constructs `TriggeredRunDeliveryDriver` from the assembly's `RunDeliveryServices`, `channel_host.rs:569` — a third output of the same seam, not a stray). Full reasoning, the 24-symbol recount (the row says 26), the three values the seam must return, and the one known wrinkle (`ChannelWorkflowState.conversations` is a concrete `ironclaw_conversations` type and must not cross back) are in PROPOSAL §12.11 D-A. **The layer flip is unblocked once the port lands.** *Original row text follows.* The file is **composition-shaped assembly living in the extension host**: it inline-constructs product's *concrete* types — `DefaultProductSurface`, `DefaultInboundTurnService`, `RebornFilesystemIdempotencyLedger`, `StaticProductInstallationResolver`, `DirectConversationCommandAdmission::new`, `ProductConversationBindingService::new`, `DeliveryCoordinator`, `RunDeliveryServices`/`Settings`/`Observer` — which by construction cannot be inverted into contracts, because a concrete implementation is exactly what a contracts crate may never name. It alone holds **26 of the 62** product symbols WS2.1 left, and after WS2.2 it is also one of only two files still naming `ProductSurfaceFailure` (it implements `ConversationBindingService` and `ProductActorUserResolver` there). **No WS2 row owns it**, and §6.8.2's shed list does not name it. Two options, both identified by WS2.1:
  - **(a) Move it to `ironclaw_composition`** — it is assembly, and §6.9.1's family rule already says product-family crates "never wire anything"; the same argument applies to a crate that will sit *below* product. Cost: composition grows, and the channel-host assembly stops living beside the channel machinery it assembles.
  - **(b) Invert the assembly behind a factory port** — `extension_host` declares what it needs as a port in `product_contracts` and composition supplies the built surface. Cost: a new port family for a single caller, which the contracts admission test's "two or more consumers" rule makes hard to justify as written.
  **The layer flip cannot happen until this is resolved either way** — `products` → `loops` makes every one of those 26 symbols an upward dependency and the crate stops compiling. This is the ordering constraint §12.1c calls "the sharpest edge in the whole restructure", and it is now the binding one.

Three in-repo numbers now describe this crate in three different units, all honest: this row's source lines, `coverage-floor.toml:229-232`'s **9,979** source / **5,440** instrumented at the slice tip, and #7018's **9,671**-line carve-out delta. The lists were right; the count was not.* Arrived: `extension_lifecycle_capabilities` + `extension_lifecycle_command`, the lifecycle product service, the `channel_config` product service (`RebornChannelConfigProductService`, extracted — 100 lines out of `channel_config.rs`'s 1,408), `webui_extension_credentials`, and the admin/operator/skill-auto-activate capability handlers. **Did not:** `product_lifecycle`, the available-extension catalog + import, pairing workflow orchestration, and `SharedCommandSurface`. Two items the row did not name arrived because they belong by charter and could not stay behind: `ironhub` (extension-hub search/info/install — extension-management UX, and the last host-side consumer of the lifecycle fixture) and `test_support/lifecycle.rs`, which builds the *product-face* service bundle.
  2. ✎ **Amended 2026-08-01 — the row's own inventory contradicts §6.8.3's charter on `product_lifecycle`, and the charter is right.** The list this row opens with reads "the #6616/#6669 arrival inventory: **`product_lifecycle`**, `available_extensions`+import, …"; §6.8.3's charter for the receiving crate reads "Owns the extension-management UX semantics; **never owns lifecycle *authority* (calls `extension_host`)**". Both cannot hold. `ExtensionLifecycleManager` **is** what it calls: it holds the operation lock, drives activation transactions through `lifecycle_restore`, and is the sole caller of `install_policy`/`removal_cleanup`/`active_publication`. Independently it is **mutually recursive** with #6930's registration pipeline — `hosted_mcp_preparation.rs` calls five of its `pub(crate)` helpers across **16 sites** and `product_lifecycle.rs` constructs `HostedMcpPreparationService` in return — so it cannot cross a crate boundary in either direction without an inversion. Same shape for the catalog (`lifecycle_restore` reads it at boot — authority, not UX) and for pairing (no separable "orchestration" module exists; what is there is the service core §6.8.2 reserves, consumed by five host modules). `SharedCommandSurface` is a *private* struct inside `channel_host.rs` and is blocked on the open `[decision]` row above.
  3. **The measured effect.** `extension_host` production files naming `ironclaw_assistant` **20 → 12** (the count first read 13; the review pass hardened the gate's scanner to resolve `#[cfg(test)] mod` chains, which reclassified `channel_host/e2e_auth_challenge.rs` — a test-only fixture wearing a production filename — out of the set). Port-inversion residue **5 → 4**: `ExtensionCredentialSetupService`'s only implementation (`webui_extension_credentials.rs`) left the crate, so the upward edge that list tracks is gone even though the port is still product-declared — the manager now carries that implementation, frozen by its own trait-residue gate. The manager arrives with a **seven-file** `ironclaw_assistant` residue of its own — and the finding is *what* those seven are: **DTOs, capability-id constants** (`ADMIN_CONFIGURATION_VIEW`, `RebornAdminConfiguration*`, `RebornChannelConnectStrategy`, the three `*_CAPABILITY_ID`s, `RebornSkillActionResponse`), **and two port-inversion residues** (the `ExtensionCredentialSetupService` port and the auth-continuation fixture wiring) — never a workflow call. The manager does not reach into product's workflow at all; it reaches for vocabulary §6.1.3 already assigns to `product_contracts` and the WS1.4 sweep did not carry. Frozen exact-match and shrink-only.
  4. **A trait-shaped gate could not have caught the thing that matters, so the split got its own.** `reborn_extension_manager_split.rs` (5 tests, all negative-probed): the host never depends on the manager **at the manifest *or* the source level and for any dependency kind, dev-dependencies included** — a fixture cycle is still a cycle in the graph the layer flip must satisfy; each half kept its own job (authority modules still in the host, product-face modules only in the manager, checked in both directions); and the manager's product residue is frozen. Its walker **panics on an I/O error** rather than skipping the entry, so a broken path cannot make the shrink-only lists pass vacuously. `reborn_extension_host_port_inversion.rs` gained a per-port **implementor crate** (its own fix-it message already said "if the implementor moved, move this row with it") plus a both-crates check so a split cannot silently *fork* a port, and its "the contract error is really in use" floor is now summed across the two halves rather than lowered.
  5. **Enumerating gates.** CLI exact-dep allowlist **13 → 14** — the `extension` and `ironhub` commands render the extension-management command surface, an edge the binary always had and the split merely names honestly. `classify-test-scope.sh`'s reborn arm (**the trap, reproduced live before the fix**: the new crate's paths classified `has_reborn_tests=false`), `reborn-crate-test-buckets.sh`'s `extension-operator` bucket and both self-tests, the `untrusted_ingress_paths_cannot_submit_host_trusted_inbound` scan roots, and a `boundary_rules()` entry. `discover-reborn-package-crates.sh` needs no allowlist entry — verified through the shipped-binary closure, not assumed. `coverage-floor.toml`'s `ironclaw_extension_host` entry was recaptured from this PR's own merged artifact in the same change (19,907/23,467 = 84.83%, the ratio *rising* across the split) and the manager's entry ratcheted from birth (4,602/5,440 = 84.60%) — the one-release percentage-floor gap of the `ironclaw_turns`/WS1.2 precedent stays closed. Bash and Python crate inventories agree at **67**. Untouched: the extension-specificity allowlist (the moved fixture kept its `test_support/` path component rather than buying two entries — a flattened name would have been a relaxation), the composition `pub use` snapshot, the struct/test-support ratchet, the `include_str!` scan, the `ProductSurface` method freeze, and `LAYER_MATRIX_EXCEPTIONS` (still 13).
  6. ✎ **Added 2026-08-02 (Wave 2 truth audit) — the split's five deliberately-deferred findings are tracked in #7011, and no row said so.** Review of #7003 surfaced five defects in the moved files, and all five sit in code the split relocated **byte-for-byte** (`git diff -M` reports `| 0` for every one), so patching them there would have destroyed the behavior-free-move claim and the rename detection that makes a ~9.7k-line split reviewable. They were correctly left out; what was missing is a pointer from the row that created them. In descending value: (1) `skill_auto_activate_capability.rs` declares `EffectKind::WriteFilesystem` but writes only a process-local `Arc<AtomicBool>` — the declared effect is false *and* the user setting does not survive a restart, and the two remedies point opposite ways, so it needs a product decision; (2) `operator_config_capability.rs`'s `tool_permission_locked` gates a `PolicyDenied` and its deny path is never exercised — the only handler test builds a tool the predicate always rejects, so inverting it or dropping `ModifyBudget` from `hard_floor_tool` leaves the suite green while an operator always-allows a hard-floor tool (exactly root `CLAUDE.md`'s "test through the caller" case); (3) that capability has no dispatch-level test at all; (4) five `.map_err(|_| …)` sites in `admin_configuration_capability.rs:145-171` drop genuinely-reachable input-validation causes; (5) the same in `webui_extension_credentials.rs:70-71`. **This row is done; #7011 is the successor and it is open.** The pattern is worth keeping: a byte-identical move PR should open one issue for what it declined to fix, and the row that moved the code should name it.
- [x] ✅ **[decision] RESOLVED 2026-08-02 (delegated authority — PROPOSAL §12.11 D-C).** ✎ **Box ticked 2026-08-04.** A `[decision]` row, ticked for the ruling, not for an implementation. Verified on the merged tree: the pipeline is still in `ironclaw_extension_host` (`src/hosted_mcp_*.rs`, five modules) and `reborn_registration_pipeline_boundary.rs`'s ownership constant still names it, so the tree matches the ruling's placement half. **Option (c)'s mechanical half is NOT built** — the four leaf helpers have not been relocated to a shared leaf module and the two stateful ones are still reached as `pub(crate)` rather than through the injected 2-method port whose forwarders already exist at `hosted_mcp_preparation.rs:587-604`. That is follow-on work with no bearing on the layer flip (it is entirely intra-crate, so it creates and removes no cross-crate edge), and it is carried forward on #7145 (successor to #7092) rather than being left as a silently-open box here. **The pipeline's home is `ironclaw_extension_host` — settled on §6.8.2's charter rather than forced by entanglement — and the coupling becomes a declared edge per option (c).** The premise that made WS2.4's placement "forced" is refuted by measurement: the helpers are module-scope **free functions**, none takes `&self`, and the pipeline never names `ExtensionLifecycleManager` — there is no object-level cycle, only a module↔module reference inside one crate. Recount: **6** helpers not 5 (`map_extension_error`, `product_lifecycle.rs:3069`, called at `hosted_mcp_manifest.rs:380`), **19** call sites not 16 (20 with the sixth), `product_lifecycle.rs` is **4,505** lines not 4,152, and the pipeline is **5** modules not 4 (`hosted_mcp_discovery_authority.rs` is matched by the same scope). Four of the six are pure leaves (75 lines) that relocate to a leaf module both sides import downward; the two stateful ones (70 lines) already take their `Arc` dependencies as explicit parameters and their 2-method port shape **already exists** as private forwarders (`hosted_mcp_preparation.rs:587-604`). Option (b) is rejected on measurement (only ~423 of 3,156 production lines are product-face; it leaves ~2,730, still over both file thresholds) **and is gate-blocked**: `activation_credentials_incomplete_response` has 4 of its 5 callers inside the pipeline, so moving it to the manager creates the `extension_host → extension_manager` back-edge `reborn_extension_manager_split.rs` exists to forbid. Option (a) is rejected because the "split feature" is smaller and better-layered than the row states — the route is `ironclaw_webui`, the modal is its frontend, the catalog projection is `ironclaw_assistant`, and the manager's whole involvement is a 5-line action dispatch plus two predicate guards — so what is actually wrong is the undeclared `pub(crate)` reach-in, which (c) fixes cheaply. **Recorded residual the helper framing hides:** the real coupling is the shared `catalog` + `operation_lock` under the documented AB-BA lock-order invariant (`hosted_mcp_preparation.rs:87-92`); no helper-level port dissolves it, and separating it is a distinct design question this ruling does not settle. **Gate correction:** the constant is `OWNED_SCOPES` (`reborn_registration_pipeline_boundary.rs:71`), not `OWNED_PREFIXES`, and it enforces vocabulary containment, not location. Full reasoning in PROPOSAL §12.11 D-C. *Original row text follows.* WS2.4 kept it in `ironclaw_extension_host`, which is what §6.8.2's own charter argues for (it is generic, it is a trust job, it writes lifecycle state). That was **forced, not chosen**: `hosted_mcp_preparation.rs` and `product_lifecycle.rs` are mutually recursive — five `pub(crate)` helpers across 16 call sites one way, a `HostedMcpPreparationService` construction the other — so registration could only move if `ExtensionLifecycleManager` moved with it, and §6.8.3 forbids the manager owning lifecycle authority. The seam therefore still runs through the feature: the WebUI route, the modal, and the catalog projection that drive registration are manager-side while the pipeline is host-side. Three options:
  - **(a) Leave it in the host and accept the split feature.** Zero work; the cost is that "where does registration live" has two answers depending on which end you enter from.
  - **(b) Decompose `product_lifecycle.rs`** into the authority workflow (host) and the product-face response building (manager), breaking the recursion first. This is the option that would also unblock the `product_lifecycle` inventory line, and it is a semantic refactor, not a move.
  - **(c) Give registration its own module boundary** inside the host with an explicit port to the lifecycle workflow, so the recursion becomes a declared edge rather than a `pub(crate)` reach-in.
  Whichever wins, `reborn_registration_pipeline_boundary.rs`'s `OWNED_PREFIXES` (today only `crates/ironclaw_extension_host/src/hosted_mcp_`) travels with the pipeline. Evidence pointer: the WS2.4 PR.

The fork is what *prevents* an `extension_host → operator` dependency, not sloppiness. Its only in-crate consumer is `available_extensions.rs`, which this row originally expected the `extension_manager` split to take — but that row **landed without it**, on the argument that the catalog is lifecycle *authority* (read by `lifecycle_restore` at boot), not extension-management UX. Flipping `REPORT_ONLY` needs all three, and none is this row's — **filed as #7093**, which also records that survivor group A may not be fixable as stated (the obvious inversion is an upward `loops → products` edge) and that the scan's printed baselines are now stale enough to mislead.

The real work is unchanged and is one slice: give `nearai-mcp` a `packages/nearai.rs` inventory module like `notion.rs` (it is the only *production* reach-in, and the one asset directory of twelve with no module), replace the two test-only manifest fixtures (github, gmail) with inline TOML, and route the composition/manager test reach-ins through `bundled_packages()`. **The `nearai_mcp.rs` fork stays**: it is what prevents an `extension_host → operator` upward edge once extension_host drops to `loops`, and the fix does not touch it — only where the manifest *text* comes from. The scope derivation's equivalence with `ApprovalInteractionScope` is pinned in product rather than left to review.

WS2.5 deliberately did not attempt it: it is a bundle-shaped inversion across four crates whose port shape D-A's own confidence note still calls open, and shipping it unverified beside a 48-file vocabulary move would have traded a measured change for an unmeasurable one. The flip's remaining scope is therefore exactly: the D-A factory port, plus #7174's adapter-registry class, then the manifest edge and the layer line in one change.
    - ✎ **2026-08-04 (WS2.6) — the D-A factory port is BUILT, and the blocker list above is now one class, not three.** Measured and executed on `0b0eb3124`. `ChannelWorkflowFactory` is declared in `ironclaw_product_contracts::channel_workflow`, implemented by `ironclaw_assistant::RebornChannelWorkflowFactory`, and injected through `GenericChannelHostDeps` exactly as the ruling specified. Both `assembly` ledger rows fell with it, and so did the two rows they gated: `EXTENSION_HOST_PRODUCTION_FILES_STILL_NAMING_PRODUCT` **5 → 3**, `WS2_PRODUCT_DEFINED_TRAIT_RESIDUE_BASELINE` **1 → 0**, `EXTENSION_HOST_FILES_STILL_NAMING_THE_WORKFLOW_ERROR` **1 → 0**. Four measured notes the ruling did not anticipate:

The host never held the durable state for its own sake; it held it to hand straight back to product.

The collision was real rather than cosmetic (one resolves conversation bindings inside the conversations domain; the other resolves product adapter refs at the product boundary), so it was renamed rather than exempted.

All three carry `String`/nothing, so the contracts ceiling is untouched and the absorbing `From` stays total and 1:1.

That was a faithful proxy only while a trait was the last thing holding the edge; with the trait residue at zero and three `adapter-registry` ledger rows still naming the crate, it would have demanded the edge be *deleted* from a tree that cannot compile without it — failing a correct state and passing an impossible one. It now keys on the trait residue **OR** the reference ledger, which is the union the ledger test already asserts from the other side. Both directions stay enforced: the edge must exist while either list is occupied, and must be gone when both are.
